The Argument Model Ontology (AMO)

http://purl.org/spar/amo
bibliographic citation
Peroni, S., Tomasi, F., Vitali, F., & Zingoni, J. (2013, January). Semantic lenses as exploration method for scholarly articles. In Italian Research Conference on Digital Libraries (pp. 118-129). Berlin, Heidelberg: Springer Berlin Heidelberg. DOI: https://doi.org/10.1007/978-3-642-54347-0_13.
code repository
https://github.com/SPAROntologies/amo
contributor
Sebastian Barzaghi (https://orcid.org/0000-0002-0799-1527)
created
2011-05-04
creator
Fabio Vitali (https://orcid.org/0000-0002-7562-5203)
Silvio Peroni (https://orcid.org/0000-0003-0530-4305)
date
2026-09-30
description
The **_Argument Model Ontology (AMO)_** is an OWL 2 DL ontology that aims to encode the [Toulmin's theory](https://doi.org/10.1017/CBO9780511840005) through OWL classes and properties, in order to describe a web of inter-linked entities that participate, with a specific role, in one or more arguments. Toulmin's model of argument focuses on the justificatory function of argumentation: rather than evaluating arguments through formal logic, it describes how people, in practice, put forward a claim and then justify it. According to this model, every practical argument can be analysed in terms of six interrelated components. Three of them are essential: the claim to be established, the evidence (or data) on which it is founded, and the warrant that bridges from the evidence to the claim. The other three may be omitted in some arguments: the backing that certifies the warrant, the qualifier that expresses the degree of certainty of the claim, and the rebuttal that states the conditions under which the claim would not hold. ![The main architecture of AMO.](amo.png) AMO models an argument through the class `amo:Argument`, whose components are instances of `amo:Claim`, `amo:Evidence`, `amo:Warrant`, `amo:Backing` and `amo:Rebuttal`, all grouped under the class `amo:ArgumentationEntity`, and of `amo:Qualifier`. An argument is linked to its components through the sub-properties of `amo:involves` (i.e., `amo:hasClaim`, `amo:hasEvidence`, `amo:hasWarrant`, `amo:hasBacking`, `amo:hasQualifier` and `amo:hasRebuttal`), and each argument must have exactly one claim and at least one evidence and one warrant. The components are, in turn, linked to one another: an evidence proves a claim (`amo:proves`) and supports a warrant (`amo:supports`), a warrant leads to a claim (`amo:leadsTo`), a backing backs a warrant (`amo:backs`), a qualifier forces a claim (`amo:forces`), and a claim is valid unless a rebuttal holds (`amo:isValidUnless`). Several of these relations are automatically inferred, by means of property chains, from the description of the roles that the components hold within the argument. ![How an argument is modelled using AMO.](argument.png) AMO is aligned with the [Citation Typing Ontology (CiTO)](http://purl.org/spar/cito), an ontology for the characterization of citations, both factually and rhetorically that forms part of [SPAR, a suite of Semantic Publishing and Referencing Ontologies](http://sparontologies.net). ## Examples of use In the following subsections, we introduce some examples to showcase how to use AMO. The prefixes that are used in all the examples provided below are defined as follows: @prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> . @prefix : <http://example.org/> . ### Describing the components of an argument Consider the argument "This ontology was developed in OWL, and since an ontology developed in OWL is a consistent OWL ontology, as stated by the OWL 2 specifications, this is presumably a consistent OWL ontology, unless a reasoner for OWL 2 proves that it is not consistent". The argument can be described from a high-level perspective by stating which argumentation entities it involves and which role each of them holds. :argument a amo:Argument ; amo:hasClaim :consistency-claim ; amo:hasEvidence :developed-in-owl ; amo:hasWarrant :owl-warrant ; amo:hasBacking :owl2-spec ; amo:hasQualifier :presumably ; amo:hasRebuttal :reasoner-rebuttal . :consistency-claim a amo:Claim . :developed-in-owl a amo:Evidence . :owl-warrant a amo:Warrant . :owl2-spec a amo:Backing . :presumably a amo:Qualifier . :reasoner-rebuttal a amo:Rebuttal . ### Describing the relations between argumentation entities The same argument can be described from a factual perspective, by stating how its argumentation entities are linked to one another. The relations between evidence and claim, warrant and claim, qualifier and claim, and claim and rebuttal can also be inferred from the high-level description through the property chains defined in AMO. :developed-in-owl amo:proves :consistency-claim ; amo:supports :owl-warrant . :owl-warrant amo:leadsTo :consistency-claim . :owl2-spec amo:backs :owl-warrant . :presumably amo:forces :consistency-claim . :consistency-claim amo:isValidUnless :reasoner-rebuttal . ## Competency Questions AMO can be used for answering several questions related to arguments and how argumentation entities are related to each other. In the following subsections, some of them are introduced together with their respective SPARQL queries. The prefixes that are used in all the SPARQL queries provided below are defined as follows: PREFIX amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> PREFIX : <http://example.org/> PREFIX rdfs: <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> ### CQ1 What is the claim of a given argument, and which evidence proves it? SELECT ?claim ?evidence WHERE { :argument amo:hasClaim ?claim ; amo:hasEvidence ?evidence . } ### CQ2 Which warrants lead to a given claim, and which backings attest them? SELECT ?warrant ?backing WHERE { ?warrant amo:leadsTo :consistency-claim . OPTIONAL { ?backing amo:backs ?warrant } } ### CQ3 What is the degree of force of a given claim, and under which rebuttals is it not valid? SELECT ?qualifier ?rebuttal WHERE { OPTIONAL { ?qualifier amo:forces :consistency-claim } OPTIONAL { :consistency-claim amo:isValidUnless ?rebuttal } }

back to ToC or Class ToC

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/Argument

comment
An argument, or practical argument according to the 'Toulmin model of argument', focuses on the justificatory function of argumentation: practical arguments first find a claim of interest, and then provide justification for it. Each argument must be analyzed according to six interrelated components: claim, evidence, warrant, backing, rebuttal and qualifier. The first three elements, 'claim', 'data' and 'warrant', are considered as the essential components of practical arguments, while the second triad, 'qualifier', 'backing', and 'rebuttal', may not be needed in some arguments.

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs: <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .
@prefix reg: <http://purl.org/linked-data/registry#> .
@prefix vann: <http://purl.org/vocab/vann/> .
@prefix vs: <http://www.w3.org/2003/06/sw-vocab-status/ns#> .
@prefix xsd: <http://www.w3.org/2001/XMLSchema#> .

amo:Argument a owl:Class ;
    rdfs:label "argument"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-argument a amo:Argument ;
        amo:hasClaim :my-claim ;
        amo:hasEvidence :my-evidence ;
        amo:hasWarrant :my-warrant ;
        amo:hasBacking :my-backing ;
        amo:hasQualifier :my-qualifier ;
        amo:hasRebuttal :my-rebuttal .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment """
An argument, or practical argument according to the 'Toulmin model of argument', focuses on the justificatory function of argumentation: practical arguments first find a claim of interest, and then provide justification for it.

Each argument must be analyzed according to six interrelated components: claim, evidence, warrant, backing, rebuttal and qualifier. 
The first three elements, 'claim', 'data' and 'warrant', are considered as the essential components of practical arguments, while the second triad, 'qualifier', 'backing', and 'rebuttal', may not be needed in some arguments.
"""@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:subClassOf [ a owl:Class ;
            owl:intersectionOf ( [ a owl:Restriction ;
                        owl:minCardinality "1"^^xsd:nonNegativeInteger ;
                        owl:onProperty amo:hasEvidence ] [ a owl:Restriction ;
                        owl:minCardinality "1"^^xsd:nonNegativeInteger ;
                        owl:onProperty amo:hasWarrant ] [ a owl:Restriction ;
                        owl:cardinality "1"^^xsd:nonNegativeInteger ;
                        owl:onProperty amo:hasClaim ] ) ] ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

An argument, or practical argument according to the 'Toulmin model of argument', focuses on the justificatory function of argumentation: practical arguments first find a claim of interest, and then provide justification for it.

Each argument must be analyzed according to six interrelated components: claim, evidence, warrant, backing, rebuttal and qualifier. 
The first three elements, 'claim', 'data' and 'warrant', are considered as the essential components of practical arguments, while the second triad, 'qualifier', 'backing', and 'rebuttal', may not be needed in some arguments.

<http://purl.org/spar/amo/Argument> <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#comment> "\nAn argument, or practical argument according to the 'Toulmin model of argument', focuses on the justificatory function of argumentation: practical arguments first find a claim of interest, and then provide justification for it.\n\nEach argument must be analyzed according to six interrelated components: claim, evidence, warrant, backing, rebuttal and qualifier. \nThe first three elements, 'claim', 'data' and 'warrant', are considered as the essential components of practical arguments, while the second triad, 'qualifier', 'backing', and 'rebuttal', may not be needed in some arguments.\n"@en .
_:n1a0a3a68be7f463a92e2a2d38d5834b0b52 <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#rest> <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#nil> .
<http://purl.org/spar/amo/Argument> <http://www.w3.org/2003/06/sw-vocab-status/ns#term_status> <http://purl.org/linked-data/registry#statusStable> .
<http://purl.org/spar/amo/Argument> <http://purl.org/vocab/vann/example> "\n    :my-argument a amo:Argument ;\n        amo:hasClaim :my-claim ;\n        amo:hasEvidence :my-evidence ;\n        amo:hasWarrant :my-warrant ;\n        amo:hasBacking :my-backing ;\n        amo:hasQualifier :my-qualifier ;\n        amo:hasRebuttal :my-rebuttal .\n    " .
<http://purl.org/spar/amo/Argument> <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#subClassOf> _:n1a0a3a68be7f463a92e2a2d38d5834b0b46 .

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/ArgumentationEntity

comment
An entity that takes part in an argument according to the 'Toulmin model of argument'.
en
is defined by
http://purl.org/spar/amo/
is equivalent to
( (

or

or
evidencec
or
rebuttalc
or
warrantc
) and
is involved inop
some
argumentc
)

argumentation entity
en
term status

example
:my-claim a amo:ArgumentationEntity ; amo:isInvolvedIn :my-argument . 

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs: <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .

amo:ArgumentationEntity a owl:Class ;
    rdfs:label "argumentation entity"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-claim a amo:ArgumentationEntity ;
        amo:isInvolvedIn :my-argument .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "An entity that takes part in an argument according to the 'Toulmin model of argument'."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    owl:equivalentClass [ a owl:Class ;
            owl:intersectionOf ( [ a owl:Class ;
                        owl:unionOf ( amo:Backing amo:Claim amo:Evidence amo:Rebuttal amo:Warrant ) ] [ a owl:Restriction ;
                        owl:onProperty amo:isInvolvedIn ;
                        owl:someValuesFrom amo:Argument ] ) ] ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

claim
c

back to ToC or Class ToC

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/Claim

comment
A fact that must be established - e.g., 'This is a consistent OWL ontology'.
en
is defined by
http://purl.org/spar/amo/
is equivalent to
(
argumentation entityc
and
is claim inop
some
argumentc
)
is in domain of
is valid unlessop
is in range of
forcesop,
leads toop,
provesop,
has claimop
label
claim
en
term status
status stable
example
:my-claim a amo:Claim ; amo:isClaimIn :my-argument ; amo:isValidUnless :my-rebuttal . 

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs: <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .
@prefix reg: <http://purl.org/linked-data/registry#> .

@prefix vs: <http://www.w3.org/2003/06/sw-vocab-status/ns#> .

amo:Claim a owl:Class ;
    rdfs:label "claim"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-claim a amo:Claim ;
        amo:isClaimIn :my-argument ;
        amo:isValidUnless :my-rebuttal .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "A fact that must be established - e.g., 'This is a consistent OWL ontology'."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    owl:equivalentClass [ a owl:Class ;
            owl:intersectionOf ( amo:ArgumentationEntity [ a owl:Restriction ;
                        owl:onProperty amo:isClaimIn ;
                        owl:someValuesFrom amo:Argument ] ) ] ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

comment
Words or phrases that express the degree of certainty of the claim such as “certainly”, “possible”, “probably”, “presumably”, etc.

http://purl.org/spar/amo/

is qualifier inop
some
argumentc

forcesop
is in range of
has qualifierop
label
qualifier

status stable

back to ToC or Class ToC

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/Rebuttal

comment
Restrictions that may be applied to the claim - 'Unless a reasoner for OWL 2 proves that it is not consistent'.
en
is defined by
http://purl.org/spar/amo/
is equivalent to
(
argumentation entityc
and
is rebuttal inop
some
argumentc
)
is in range of
is valid unlessop,
has rebuttalop
label
rebuttal
en
example
:my-rebuttal a amo:Rebuttal ; amo:isRebuttalIn :my-argument . :my-claim amo:isValidUnless :my-rebuttal .
term status
status stable

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .

amo:Rebuttal a owl:Class ;
    rdfs:label "rebuttal"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-rebuttal a amo:Rebuttal ;
        amo:isRebuttalIn :my-argument .

    :my-claim 
        amo:isValidUnless :my-rebuttal .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "Restrictions that may be applied to the claim - 'Unless a reasoner for OWL 2 proves that it is not consistent'."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    owl:equivalentClass [ a owl:Class ;
            owl:intersectionOf ( amo:ArgumentationEntity [ a owl:Restriction ;
                        owl:onProperty amo:isRebuttalIn ;
                        owl:someValuesFrom amo:Argument ] ) ] ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

warrant

back to ToC or Class ToC

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/Warrant

comment
A statement bridging from the evidence to the claim - e.g., 'An ontology developed in OWL is a consistent OWL ontology'.
en
is defined by
http://purl.org/spar/amo/
is equivalent to
(
argumentation entityc
and
is warrant inop
some
argumentc
)
is in domain of
leads toop
is in range of
backsop,
supportsop,
has warrantop
label
warrant
en
example
:my-warrant a amo:Warrant ; amo:isWarrantIn :my-argument ; amo:leadsTo :my-claim .
term status
status stable

involves
op

back to ToC or Object Property ToC

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/involves

comment
An argument involves an argumentation entity (claim, evidence, warrant, backing, rebuttal) or a qualifier.
en
domain
argumentc
range
(
argumentation entityc
or
qualifierc
)
is defined by
http://purl.org/spar/amo/
is inverse of
is involved inop
label
involves
en
term status
status stable
example
:my-argument a amo:Argument ; amo:involves :my-claim, :my-evidence, :my-warrant .
note
This property and its sub-properties serve to describe an argument from an high-level perspective (i.e., which argumentation entities take part in it and what role they hold) rather than from a factual point of view (i.e., how argumentation entities are inter-linked between them).
en

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs: <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .
@prefix reg: <http://purl.org/linked-data/registry#> .
@prefix skos: <http://www.w3.org/2004/02/skos/core#> .
@prefix vann: <http://purl.org/vocab/vann/> .
@prefix vs: <http://www.w3.org/2003/06/sw-vocab-status/ns#> .

amo:involves a owl:ObjectProperty ;
    rdfs:label "involves"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-argument a amo:Argument ;
        amo:involves :my-claim, 
            :my-evidence, 
            :my-warrant .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "An argument involves an argumentation entity (claim, evidence, warrant, backing, rebuttal) or a qualifier."@en ;
    rdfs:domain amo:Argument ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:range [ a owl:Class ;
            owl:unionOf ( amo:ArgumentationEntity amo:Qualifier ) ] ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable ;
    skos:note "This property and its sub-properties serve to describe an argument from an high-level perspective (i.e., which argumentation entities take part in it and what role they hold) rather than from a factual point of view (i.e., how argumentation entities are inter-linked between them)."@en .

An argumentative entity linked to an argument in which it is considered a backing.

argumentc

@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .

amo:isBackingIn a owl:ObjectProperty ;
    rdfs:label "is backing in"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-backing 
        amo:isBackingIn :my-argument .

    :my-argument a amo:Argument .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "An argumentative entity linked to an argument in which it is considered a backing."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:subPropertyOf amo:isInvolvedIn ;
    owl:inverseOf amo:hasBacking ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

[] a owl:AllDisjointProperties ;
    owl:members ( amo:isBackingIn amo:isClaimIn amo:isEvidenceIn amo:isQualifierIn amo:isRebuttalIn amo:isWarrantIn ) .

is evidence in
op

back to ToC or Object Property ToC

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/isEvidenceIn

comment
An argumentative entity linked to an argument in which it is considered an evidence.
en
is sub property of
is involved inop
domain
evidencec
range
argumentc
is disjoint with
is backing inop,
is claim inop,
is qualifier inop,
is rebuttal inop,
is warrant inop
is defined by
http://purl.org/spar/amo/
is inverse of
has evidenceop
label
is evidence in
en
example
:my-evidence amo:isEvidenceIn :my-argument . :my-argument a amo:Argument .
term status
status stable

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs: <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .
@prefix reg: <http://purl.org/linked-data/registry#> .
@prefix vann: <http://purl.org/vocab/vann/> .
@prefix vs: <http://www.w3.org/2003/06/sw-vocab-status/ns#> .

amo:isEvidenceIn a owl:ObjectProperty ;
    rdfs:label "is evidence in"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-evidence 
        amo:isEvidenceIn :my-argument .

    :my-argument a amo:Argument .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "An argumentative entity linked to an argument in which it is considered an evidence."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:subPropertyOf amo:isInvolvedIn ;
    owl:inverseOf amo:hasEvidence ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

[] a owl:AllDisjointProperties ;
    owl:members ( amo:isBackingIn amo:isClaimIn amo:isEvidenceIn amo:isQualifierIn amo:isRebuttalIn amo:isWarrantIn ) .

is valid unless
op

back to ToC or Object Property ToC

IRI: http://purl.org/spar/amo/isValidUnless

comment
A claim that is restricted by a rebuttal of an argument.
en
is sub property of
relates toop,
is disputed byop
domain
claimc
range
rebuttalc
property chain
is claim inop
o
has rebuttalop
is defined by
http://purl.org/spar/amo/
label
is valid unless
en
note
By means of the property chain defined, the assertion that links a claim to the relative rebuttal is automatically inferred starting from the high-level description of an argument (realized through the sub-properties of 'involves' and their inverse properties).
en
example
:my-claim a amo:Claim ; amo:isValidUnless :my-rebuttal . :my-rebuttal a amo:Rebuttal .
term status
status stable

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix cito: <http://purl.org/spar/cito/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .
@prefix rdfs: <http://www.w3.org/2000/01/rdf-schema#> .
@prefix reg: <http://purl.org/linked-data/registry#> .
@prefix skos: <http://www.w3.org/2004/02/skos/core#> .
@prefix vann: <http://purl.org/vocab/vann/> .
@prefix vs: <http://www.w3.org/2003/06/sw-vocab-status/ns#> .

amo:isValidUnless a owl:ObjectProperty ;
    rdfs:label "is valid unless"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-claim a amo:Claim ;
        amo:isValidUnless :my-rebuttal .

    :my-rebuttal a amo:Rebuttal .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "A claim that is restricted by a rebuttal of an argument."@en ;
    rdfs:domain amo:Claim ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:range amo:Rebuttal ;
    rdfs:subPropertyOf amo:relatesTo,
        cito:isDisputedBy ;
    owl:propertyChainAxiom ( amo:isClaimIn amo:hasRebuttal ) ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable ;
    skos:note "By means of the property chain defined, the assertion that links a claim to the relative rebuttal is automatically inferred starting from the high-level description of an argument (realized through the sub-properties of 'involves' and their inverse properties)."@en .

comment
An argumentative entity linked to an argument in which it is considered a warrant.

is involved inop
domain
warrantc
range
argumentc
is disjoint with

http://purl.org/spar/amo/
is inverse of
has warrantop
label
is warrant in
en
example

term status
status stable

amo:isWarrantIn a owl:ObjectProperty ;
    rdfs:label "is warrant in"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-warrant 
        amo:isWarrantIn :my-argument .

    :my-argument a amo:Argument .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "An argumentative entity linked to an argument in which it is considered a warrant."@en ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:subPropertyOf amo:isInvolvedIn ;
    owl:inverseOf amo:hasWarrant ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

[] a owl:AllDisjointProperties ;
    owl:members ( amo:isBackingIn amo:isClaimIn amo:isEvidenceIn amo:isQualifierIn amo:isRebuttalIn amo:isWarrantIn ) .

example

    :my-claim a amo:Claim .""" ;

@prefix amo: <http://purl.org/spar/amo/> .
@prefix owl: <http://www.w3.org/2002/07/owl#> .
@prefix rdf: <http://www.w3.org/1999/02/22-rdf-syntax-ns#> .

amo:relatesTo a owl:ObjectProperty,
        owl:SymmetricProperty ;
    rdfs:label "relates to"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-evidence a amo:Evidence ;
        amo:relatesTo :my-claim .

    :my-claim a amo:Claim .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "An argumentation entity relates to another one in some way - for example, it is the evidence that is used to prove a claim of an argument."@en ;
    rdfs:domain [ a owl:Class ;
            owl:unionOf ( amo:ArgumentationEntity amo:Qualifier ) ] ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:range amo:ArgumentationEntity ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .

amo:supports a owl:ObjectProperty ;
    rdfs:label "supports"@en ;
    vann:example """
    :my-evidence a amo:Evidence ;
        amo:supports :my-warrant .

    :my-warrant a amo:Warrant .
    """ ;
    rdfs:comment "An evidence that supports a warrant of an argument."@en ;
    rdfs:domain amo:Evidence ;
    rdfs:isDefinedBy amo: ;
    rdfs:range amo:Warrant ;
    rdfs:subPropertyOf amo:relatesTo,
        cito:givesSupportTo ;
    vs:term_status reg:statusStable .
